Theorem 218.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For convergence analysis pairs (A,B) in quantum theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 166 and the invariant I(A,B) = 984 mod 4.

Proof: The convergence analysis estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 166 + O(1/n^984). Summing over all modes and applying the quantum convergence analysis criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.

Theorem 218.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the quantum structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 34. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 102 and converges at rate O(1/n^3).

Proof: Consider the structural decomposition operator L on the quantum space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 34 for all k. By the structural decomposition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 102. QED.

Theorem 218.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Under the optimality condition assumption on quantum structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 875. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 791 with relations in degree 1.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 875 is immediate from the optimality condition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the quantum structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 791. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
